=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given two integer arrays, skill and mana, of length n and m, respectively.
In a laboratory, n wizards must brew m potions in order. Each potion has a mana capacity mana[j] and must pass through all the wizards sequentially to be brewed properly. The time taken by the i^th wizard on the j^th potion is time_ij = skill[i] * mana[j].
Since the brewing process is delicate, a potion must be passed to the next wizard immediately after the current wizard completes their work. This means the timing must be synchronized so that each wizard begins working on a potion exactly when it arrives. ​
Return the minimum amount of time required for the potions to be brewed properly.
 
Example 1:

Input: skill = [1,5,2,4], mana = [5,1,4,2]
Output: 110
Explanation:



Potion Number
Start time
Wizard 0 done by
Wizard 1 done by
Wizard 2 done by
Wizard 3 done by


0
0
5
30
40
60


1
52
53
58
60
64


2
54
58
78
86
102


3
86
88
98
102
110



As an example for why wizard 0 cannot start working on the 1^st potion before time t = 52, consider the case where the wizards started preparing the 1^st potion at time t = 50. At time t = 58, wizard 2 is done with the 1^st potion, but wizard 3 will still be working on the 0^th potion till time t = 60.

Example 2:

Input: skill = [1,1,1], mana = [1,1,1]
Output: 5
Explanation:

Preparation of the 0^th potion begins at time t = 0, and is completed by time t = 3.
Preparation of the 1^st potion begins at time t = 1, and is completed by time t = 4.
Preparation of the 2^nd potion begins at time t = 2, and is completed by time t = 5.


Example 3:

Input: skill = [1,2,3,4], mana = [1,2]
Output: 21

 
Constraints:

n == skill.length
m == mana.length
1 <= n, m <= 5000
1 <= mana[i], skill[i] <= 5000

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.

```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

start = [0]*m
for i in range(1,m):
    max_time = 0
    for j in range(n):
        max_time = max(max_time, prefix[j+1]*mana[i-1] - prefix[j]*mana[i])
    start[i] = start[i-1] + max_time
return start[-1] + prefix[-1]*mana[-1]
```

Let's consider performance. O(n*m) = 25M. We can optimize by local variables:
```
class Solution:
    def minTime(self, skill: List[int], mana: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(skill)
        m = len(mana)
        pref = [0]*(n+1)
        for i, s in enumerate(skill):
            pref[i+1] = pref[i] + s
        start = 0
        for j in range(m-1):
            a = mana[j]
            b = mana[j+1]
            best = 0
            # i from 0 to n-1
            # pref[i+1]*a - pref[i]*b
            # Could initialize with i=0: pref[1]*a - pref[0]*b = skill[0]*a
            for i in range(n):
                val = pref[i+1]*a - pref[i]*b
                if val > best:
                    best = val
            start += best
        return start + pref[n] * mana[-1]
```
This is simple. But note `best=0` is safe because i=0 val=skill[0]*a >0. If n>=1. Good.

Can we reduce multiplications by using recurrence for val? Since pref[i+1]=pref[i]+skill[i]. val = pref[i]*(a-b)+skill[i]*a. But still multiplication. Maybe if a==b, val = skill[i]*a, max = max_skill*a. But no need. Could optimize using local arrays and while loop? For 25M, for loop with range and list indexing okay. Now, final code:
```
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minTime(self, skill: List[int], mana: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(skill)
        m = len(mana)
        pref = [0] * (n + 1)
        for i, s in enumerate(skill):
            pref[i + 1] = pref[i] + s

        start = 0
        for j in range(m - 1):
            a = mana[j]
            b = mana[j + 1]
            best = 0
            # local variables for speed
            p = pref
            for i in range(n):
                val = p[i + 1] * a - p[i] * b
                if val > best:
                    best = val
            start += best

        return start + pref[n] * mana[-1]
```
Could move `p = pref` outside loop to avoid assignment each iteration:
```
p = pref
for j in range(m - 1):
    ...
    for i in range(n):
        val = p[i+1]*a - p[i]*b
```
Also maybe `rng = range(n)` outside? Range object reusable:
```
rng = range(n)
for j in range(m - 1):
    ...
    for i in rng:
```
Range object can be iterated multiple times. Good.
```
p = pref
rng = range(n)
for j in range(m - 1):
    a = mana[j]; b = mana[j+1]
    best = 0
    for i in rng:
       val = p[i+1]*a - p[i]*b
       if val > best: best = val
    start += best
```
This is fine.

We need compute for each transition j:
B_j = max_{i=0..n-1} (P_{i+1} * a_j - P_i * b_j), where a_j=mana[j], b_j=mana[j+1].
P_i cumulative skill. Let s_i = skill[i], P_{i+1}=P_i+s_i.
B_j = max_i (P_i (a_j - b_j) + s_i a_j).

This is maximum of lines y = P_i * x + s_i * a? Wait x = a_j - b_j, but coefficient s_i*a_j also depends on a_j. Not simply lines in one variable because intercept depends on a_j. But can rewrite in terms of ratio r = a_j / b_j? B_j = b_j * max_i (P_{i+1} * (a_j/b_j) - P_i) if b_j>0. Let r_j = a_j/b_j. Then B_j = b_j * max_i (P_{i+1} r - P_i). Since P_{i+1}=P_i+s_i. This is max of lines in r with slope P_{i+1} and intercept -P_i. r is positive rational. Could build upper hull of lines over r values (mana ratios) to query in O(log n) or O(1) if sorted. But m,n 5000, not needed. But maybe if constraints were 1e5, need. Here not.

Let's see if B_j can be computed with prefix maxima in O(n+m) because r values are arbitrary but maybe slopes P_{i+1} increasing and intercepts -P_i decreasing? Let's analyze. Lines L_i(r) = P_{i+1} r - P_i. Slopes P_{i+1} strictly increasing. Intercepts -P_i strictly decreasing (more negative). For increasing slopes and decreasing intercepts, the upper envelope might be all lines? Let's check. For r>0. Difference L_{i+1}(r) - L_i(r) = (P_{i+2}-P_{i+1})r - (P_{i+1}-P_i) = s_{i+1} r - s_i. This can be positive or negative depending on r. So not all. Upper envelope can be computed with convex hull. But n small.

Could there be a simpler recurrence using "max of prefix"? Let's express B_j = max_i (P_{i+1} a - P_i b). Since P_{i+1}=P_i+s_i. If a >= b, coefficient P_i nonnegative, so B_j maybe max over i of P_i(a-b)+s_i a. Since P_i increasing and s_i positive. The term could be maximized at largest i? Not necessarily because s_i varies. Example skill=[100,1], a=b=1: B=max(100,1)=100 at i=0, not largest. So no.

Given constraints, O(n*m) is fine.

Now, let's maybe consider if there is a way to make code more concise:
```
class Solution:
    def minTime(self, skill: List[int], mana: List[int]) -> int:
        pref = [0]
        for s in skill:
            pref.append(pref[-1] + s)
        start = 0
        for j in range(len(mana)-1):
            a, b = mana[j], mana[j+1]
            start += max(pref[i+1]*a - pref[i]*b for i in range(len(skill)))
        return start + pref[-1]*mana[-1]
```
This is elegant but generator max maybe slower. Since constraints okay, but to be safe, manual loop. Final code can be a bit longer.

```
rng = range(n)
for j in range(m - 1):
    a = mana[j]
    b = mana[j + 1]
    best = 0
    for i in rng:
        val = pref[i + 1] * a - pref[i] * b
        if val > best:
            best = val
    start += best
```

Constraint for machine i: start_{i,j+1} >= completion_{i,j}. So S_{j+1} + sum_{h<i} p_{h,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{h<=i} p_{h,j}. Thus S_{j+1} - S_j >= sum_{h<=i} p_{h,j} - sum_{h<i} p_{h,j+1}. Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "minimum amount of time required" if there is a "no-wait" constraint and the recurrence should use `max_i` over `i=0..n-1` but maybe also `i=n` for completion after last wizard? Let's double-check. For no-wait flow shop, the delay between starts of job j and j+1 is often:
`d_{j,j+1} = max_{i=1..n} ( sum_{k=i}^n p_{k,j} - sum_{k=i+1}^n p_{k,j+1} )`
where p_{k,j} is processing time on machine k. Let's map. If machines 1..n, start of job j on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j}. Completion on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

Is it always possible to avoid blocking with the recurrence? Yes, because constraints ensure for every job j and wizard i, start_{i+1,j} = completion_{i,j} >= completion_{i+1,j-1}. Thus downstream free. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "minimum amount of time required" if there is a "no-wait" constraint and the recurrence should use `max_i` over `i=0..n-1` but maybe also `i=n` for completion after last wizard? Let's double-check. For no-wait flow shop, the delay between starts of job j and j+1 is often:
`d_{j,j+1} = max_{i=1..n} ( sum_{k=i}^n p_{k,j} - sum_{k=i+1}^n p_{k,j+1} )`
where p_{k,j} is processing time on machine k. Let's map. If machines 1..n, start of job j on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j}. Completion on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "minimum amount of time required" if there is a "no-wait" constraint and the recurrence should use `max_i` over `i=0..n-1` but maybe also `i=n` for completion after last wizard? Let's double-check. For no-wait flow shop, the delay between starts of job j and j+1 is often:
`d_{j,j+1} = max_{i=1..n} ( sum_{k=i}^n p_{k,j} - sum_{k=i+1}^n p_{k,j+1} )`
where p_{k,j} is processing time on machine k. Let's map. If machines 1..n, start of job j on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j}. Completion on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

Is it always possible to avoid blocking with the recurrence? Yes, because constraints ensure for every job j and wizard i, start_{i+1,j} = completion_{i,j} >= completion_{i+1,j-1}. Thus downstream free. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "minimum amount of time required" if there is a "no-wait" constraint and the recurrence should use `max_i` over `i=0..n-1` but maybe also `i=n` for completion after last wizard? Let's double-check. For no-wait flow shop, the delay between starts of job j and j+1 is often:
`d_{j,j+1} = max_{i=1..n} ( sum_{k=i}^n p_{k,j} - sum_{k=i+1}^n p_{k,j+1} )`
where p_{k,j} is processing time on machine k. Let's map. If machines 1..n, start of job j on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j}. Completion on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "minimum amount of time required" if there is a "no-wait" constraint and the recurrence should use `max_i` over `i=0..n-1` but maybe also `i=n` for completion after last wizard? Let's double-check. For no-wait flow shop, the delay between starts of job j and j+1 is often:
`d_{j,j+1} = max_{i=1..n} ( sum_{k=i}^n p_{k,j} - sum_{k=i+1}^n p_{k,j+1} )`
where p_{k,j} is processing time on machine k. Let's map. If machines 1..n, start of job j on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j}. Completion on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "minimum amount of time required" if there is a "no-wait" constraint and the recurrence should use `max_i` over `i=0..n-1` but maybe also `i=n` for completion after last wizard? Let's double-check. For no-wait flow shop, the delay between starts of job j and j+1 is often:
`d_{j,j+1} = max_{i=1..n} ( sum_{k=i}^n p_{k,j} - sum_{k=i+1}^n p_{k,j+1} )`
where p_{k,j} is processing time on machine k. Let's map. If machines 1..n, start of job j on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j}. Completion on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "minimum amount of time required" if there is a "no-wait" constraint and the recurrence should use `max_i` over `i=0..n-1` but maybe also `i=n` for completion after last wizard? Let's double-check. For no-wait flow shop, the delay between starts of job j and j+1 is often:
`d_{j,j+1} = max_{i=1..n} ( sum_{k=i}^n p_{k,j} - sum_{k=i+1}^n p_{k,j+1} )`
where p_{k,j} is processing time on machine k. Let's map. If machines 1..n, start of job j on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j}. Completion on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

Let's double-check. For no-wait flow shop, the delay between starts of job j and j+1 is often:
`d_{j,j+1} = max_{i=1..n} ( sum_{k=i}^n p_{k,j} - sum_{k=i+1}^n p_{k,j+1} )`
where p_{k,j} is processing time on machine k. Let's map. If machines 1..n, start of job j on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j}. Completion on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "minimum amount of time required" if there is a "no-wait" constraint and the recurrence should use `max_i` over `i=0..n-1` but maybe also `i=n` for completion after last wizard? Let's double-check. For no-wait flow shop, the delay between starts of job j and j+1 is often:
`d_{j,j+1} = max_{i=1..n} ( sum_{k=i}^n p_{k,j} - sum_{k=i+1}^n p_{k,j+1} )`
where p_{k,j} is processing time on machine k. Let's map. If machines 1..n, start of job j on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j}. Completion on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

In no-wait flow shop with blocking, if downstream busy, the job cannot leave, so upstream machine blocked. Our schedule avoids all blocking by ensuring downstream free at completion. Is it always possible to avoid blocking with the recurrence? Yes, because constraints ensure for every job j and wizard i, start_{i+1,j} = completion_{i,j} >= completion_{i+1,j-1}. Thus downstream free. Good.

In no-wait flow shop with blocking, if downstream busy, the job cannot leave, so upstream machine blocked. Our schedule avoids all blocking by ensuring downstream free at completion. Is it always possible to avoid blocking with the recurrence? Yes, because constraints ensure for every job j and wizard i, start_{i+1,j} = completion_{i,j} >= completion_{i+1,j-1}. Thus downstream free. Good.

Let's double-check. For no-wait flow shop, the delay between starts of job j and j+1 is often:
`d_{j,j+1} = max_{i=1..n} ( sum_{k=i}^n p_{k,j} - sum_{k=i+1}^n p_{k,j+1} )`
where p_{k,j} is processing time on machine k. Let's map. If machines 1..n, start of job j on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j}. Completion on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "minimum amount of time required" if there is a "no-wait" constraint and the recurrence should use `max_i` over `i=0..n-1` but maybe also `i=n` for completion after last wizard? Let's double-check. For no-wait flow shop, the delay between starts of job j and j+1 is often:
`d_{j,j+1} = max_{i=1..n} ( sum_{k=i}^n p_{k,j} - sum_{k=i+1}^n p_{k,j+1} )`
where p_{k,j} is processing time on machine k. Let's map. If machines 1..n, start of job j on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j}. Completion on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

In no-wait flow shop with blocking, if downstream busy, the job cannot leave, so upstream machine blocked. Our schedule avoids all blocking by ensuring downstream free at completion. Is it always possible to avoid blocking with the recurrence? Yes, because constraints ensure for every job j and wizard i, start_{i+1,j} = completion_{i,j} >= completion_{i+1,j-1}. Thus downstream free. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "minimum amount of time required" if there is a "no-wait" constraint and the recurrence should use `max_i` over `i=0..n-1` but maybe also `i=n` for completion after last wizard? Let's double-check. For no-wait flow shop, the delay between starts of job j and j+1 is often:
`d_{j,j+1} = max_{i=1..n} ( sum_{k=i}^n p_{k,j} - sum_{k=i+1}^n p_{k,j+1} )`
where p_{k,j} is processing time on machine k. Let's map. If machines 1..n, start of job j on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j}. Completion on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

Now, let's maybe think about if there is a possible issue with "minimum amount of time required" if there is a "no-wait" constraint and the recurrence should use `max_i` over `i=0..n-1` but maybe also `i=n` for completion after last wizard? Let's double-check. For no-wait flow shop, the delay between starts of job j and j+1 is often:
`d_{j,j+1} = max_{i=1..n} ( sum_{k=i}^n p_{k,j} - sum_{k=i+1}^n p_{k,j+1} )`
where p_{k,j} is processing time on machine k. Let's map. If machines 1..n, start of job j on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j}. Completion on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

`d_{j,j+1} = max_{i=1..n} ( sum_{k=i}^n p_{k,j} - sum_{k=i+1}^n p_{k,j+1} )`
where p_{k,j} is processing time on machine k. Let's map. If machines 1..n, start of job j on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j}. Completion on machine i = S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. Constraint for machine i: S_{j+1} + sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1} >= S_j + sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j}. So delay >= sum_{k=1}^{i} p_{k,j} - sum_{k=1}^{i-1} p_{k,j+1}. For i=1..n. This matches our i=0..n-1 with prefix. Good. There is no i=n+1. Good.

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def minTime(self, skill: List[int], mana: List[int]) -> int:
        n = len(skill)
        m = len(mana)

        pref = [0] * (n + 1)
        for i in range(n):
            pref[i + 1] = pref[i] + skill[i]

        start = 0
        rng = range(n)

        for j in range(m - 1):
            a = mana[j]
            b = mana[j + 1]
            best = 0

            for i in rng:
                cur = pref[i + 1] * a - pref[i] * b
                if cur > best:
                    best = cur

            start += best

        return start + pref[n] * mana[-1]
```
